    vulnerability assessment or penetration test.[K[1B[K[1B## 9. Conclusion[K[1B[K[1BThis project demonstrated the basic workflow of network reconnaissance[K[1Busing DNS queries, HTTP connectivity checks, TCP port scanning, web[K
technology fingerprinting, Netcat, and OpenSSL.[K
[K
The assessment resolved testphp.vulnweb.com to IPv4 address[K
44.228.249.3. However, HTTP and HTTPS requests timed out, Nmap reported[K
the 100 selected TCP ports as filtered, and the focused scan reported[K
ports 80 and 443 as filtered. WhatWeb could not identify web technologies,[K
the Netcat attempt failed during hostname lookup, and the TLS test[K
timed out before a handshake was captured.[K
[K
The primary learning outcome was that reconnaissance findings must[K
be based on observed evidence. DNS resolution alone does not prove[K
that a web service is reachable. Likewise, a filtered port does not[K
prove that a port is closed, and a failed fingerprinting attempt[K
does not identify a vulnerability.[K
[K
Further testing should be performed only when connectivity is available[K
and within the authorized scope of the training assessment.[K

# OWASP Juice Shop Security Assessment

Domain: Cyber Security
Level: Intermediate
Project Number: 1
Target: https://demo.owasp-juice.shop/

## Objective
Assess the intentionally vulnerable OWASP Juice Shop training
application, document at least five distinct vulnerabilities,
collect genuine evidence, and recommend mitigations.

## Contents
report/      Report draft and final report
screenshots/ Genuine screenshots of testing and results
evidence/    Sanitized supporting evidence
notes/       Testing notes and findings register

## Evidence policy
Record only findings verified during testing.
Do not fabricate screenshots, responses, or results.
Redact credentials, tokens, and personal information.

# Vulnerability Findings Register

**Project:** Intermediate Project 1: OWASP Juice Shop Security Assessment

**Assessment target:** [http://localhost:3000](http://localhost:3000)

**Assessment type:** Authorized security testing of a local OWASP Juice Shop training application

**Testing tools:** Kali Linux, Mozilla Firefox, Burp Suite Community Edition, OWASP Juice Shop

**Assessment scope:** Search functionality, login and authentication behaviour, basket functionality, public information pages, and application challenge tracking.

## Assessment Summary

The assessment involved interacting with the OWASP Juice Shop web application and observing its behaviour through Firefox and Burp Suite HTTP history.

The supplied screenshots document search input testing, a browser alert associated with an XSS test, basket contents, login attempts, HTTP requests and responses, public information pages, and the challenge scoreboard.

The scoreboard screenshot (`screenshots/23.png`) indicates that four challenges had been solved at the time of capture. Challenge completion is evidence of progress through the training application and does not, by itself, establish four independently verified security vulnerabilities.

One XSS issue appears to be demonstrated by the browser alert shown in `screenshots/06.png`. The remaining investigation areas require additional evidence before vulnerabilities can be confirmed.

---

## Finding 1: Cross-Site Scripting (XSS)

**Title:** JavaScript execution through the search input

**Category:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS), OWASP Injection

**Affected feature:** Product search functionality

**Steps performed:**

1. Opened the OWASP Juice Shop homepage in Firefox.
2. Entered search terms into the search field and observed the resulting page.
3. Tested a crafted HTML/JavaScript input in the search field within the local training application.
4. Observed the browser's response to the crafted input.
5. Reviewed application traffic using Burp Suite HTTP history.

**Expected behaviour:**

The application should treat user-supplied search text as data and display it safely. Input must not be interpreted as executable JavaScript or HTML capable of executing scripts.

**Actual observed behaviour:**

The screenshot `screenshots/06.png` shows a browser alert associated with `localhost:3000` displaying the text `xss`. The search-related screenshots (`screenshots/02.png` and `screenshots/03.png`) document the search interaction.

This is evidence of JavaScript execution associated with the search testing flow. The precise execution path and XSS subtype should be confirmed from the corresponding request and application behaviour before assigning a definitive subtype.

**Evidence filename:**

- `screenshots/02.png`: Normal product search.
- `screenshots/03.png`: Crafted search input and search results.
- `screenshots/06.png`: Browser alert showing `xss`.
- `screenshots/01.png`: Burp Suite HTTP history and selected request.

**Security impact:**

If the behaviour is reproducible for other users through an application-accessible input, an attacker may be able to execute JavaScript in the application's origin. Depending on the application's security controls and the victim's session, this could enable page manipulation, phishing, or access to information available to the injected script.

The demonstrated impact in the supplied evidence is JavaScript execution in the local training application. No theft of credentials or session tokens is demonstrated.

**Severity and justification:**

Provisional severity: Medium.

The observed script execution supports an XSS finding in the training application. A production severity assessment would require confirming exploitability, affected users, the execution context, and the practical impact. No formal CVSS score has been calculated.

**Recommended mitigation:**

- Apply context-aware output encoding to untrusted data.
- Avoid unsafe DOM APIs and inserting untrusted strings as executable HTML.
- Use safe text-rendering methods such as `textContent` where appropriate.
- Implement a restrictive Content Security Policy as a defence-in-depth measure.
- Add regression tests to ensure crafted search input is rendered safely.

**Status:** Apparent XSS demonstrated in the local lab. Confirm the exact execution path and affected code before finalising the technical subtype.

---

## Finding 2: Broken Access Control

**Title:** Access-control enforcement review of basket resources

**Category:** Broken Access Control, Authorization

**Affected feature:** Shopping basket functionality

**Steps performed:**

1. Opened the Juice Shop application in Firefox.
2. Navigated to the basket page.
3. Observed the products displayed in the basket.
4. Reviewed related application traffic in Burp Suite HTTP history.

**Expected behaviour:**

The server should enforce authorization for every protected resource and action. A user should only be able to view or modify resources that their account is authorized to access.

**Actual observed behaviour:**

The screenshots `screenshots/08.png` and `screenshots/09.png` show a basket containing product entries and associated controls.

The supplied evidence demonstrates basket functionality for the session being used. It does not demonstrate that one user can access or modify another user's basket, nor does it show a successful authorization bypass.

**Evidence filename:**

- `screenshots/08.png`: Basket containing product entries.
- `screenshots/09.png`: Basket view.
- `screenshots/10.png`: Burp Suite HTTP history.

**Security impact:**

No unauthorized access or modification has been demonstrated by the supplied evidence. If a resource-level authorization weakness were confirmed, it could potentially expose private basket information or allow unauthorized changes to another user's data.

**Severity and justification:**

Not assigned. A severity rating would be premature without evidence of an authorization failure.

**Recommended mitigation:**

- Enforce authorization checks on the server for every protected request.
- Verify resource ownership before returning or modifying basket data.
- Do not rely solely on client-side controls or user-supplied resource identifiers.
- Add automated tests for cross-account access attempts.

**Status:** Not confirmed. The evidence shows normal basket functionality, but does not establish a broken access-control vulnerability.

---

## Finding 3: Authentication Weakness

**Title:** Login failure and authentication error-handling review

**Category:** Authentication

**Affected feature:** Login form and authentication endpoint

**Steps performed:**

1. Opened the Juice Shop login page.
2. Entered test account credentials and submitted login attempts.
3. Observed the application's response to unsuccessful authentication.
4. Reviewed login-related requests and responses in Burp Suite HTTP history.

**Expected behaviour:**

The application should reject invalid credentials, avoid creating an authenticated session after a failed login, handle errors safely, and apply appropriate protections against automated password guessing.

**Actual observed behaviour:**

The screenshots `screenshots/11.png`, `screenshots/12.png`, and `screenshots/14.png` through `screenshots/17.png` document the login interface and different input states. The accompanying Burp screenshots show authentication-related traffic.

The evidence includes a visible login error and unsuccessful login behaviour. It does not establish an authentication bypass, successful login with invalid credentials, or the absence of effective rate limiting.

**Evidence filename:**

- `screenshots/11.png`: Login page.
- `screenshots/12.png`: Login and account registration interface.
- `screenshots/13.png`: Burp Suite request and response evidence.
- `screenshots/17.png`: Login form displaying an error after unusual input.

**Security impact:**

No authentication compromise has been demonstrated. An actual authentication weakness could permit unauthorized account access or facilitate password guessing, depending on the failure being verified.

**Severity and justification:**

Not assigned. The observed failed-login behaviour alone does not constitute an authentication vulnerability.

**Recommended mitigation:**

- Use secure password hashing and verification.
- Apply appropriate rate limiting and monitoring to authentication endpoints.
- Maintain secure session handling and invalidate sessions when necessary.
- Return safe, consistent authentication errors.
- Test authentication controls using controlled, documented test cases.

**Status:** Not confirmed. Failed-login behaviour was observed, but no authentication bypass or other specific authentication weakness has been demonstrated.

---

## Finding 4: Injection and SQL Injection

**Title:** Input-handling behaviour of the login endpoint

**Category:** Injection, Input Validation

**Affected feature:** Login form and authentication request processing

**Steps performed:**

1. Submitted a normal failed-login attempt using the test account's email and an incorrect password.
2. Submitted a separate login attempt using a single quote character in the email field and an incorrect password.
3. Observed the application's response.
4. Reviewed the corresponding authentication traffic in Burp Suite HTTP history.
5. Compared the observed behaviour with the normal failed-login response.

**Expected behaviour:**

The application should process user input safely, reject invalid authentication attempts, and avoid exposing internal implementation details or allowing input to alter the intended database query.

**Actual observed behaviour:**

The screenshots document login attempts and related HTTP traffic. `screenshots/17.png` appears to show an error associated with unusual input, while `screenshots/13.png`, `screenshots/18.png`, and `screenshots/22.png` document Burp Suite traffic.

The available screenshots do not conclusively establish that the error originated from a database query, that SQL query logic was altered, or that authentication was bypassed.

A malformed-input error may indicate an input-handling problem, but it is not sufficient proof of SQL injection.

**Evidence filename:**

- `screenshots/13.png`: Authentication request and response.
- `screenshots/17.png`: Login error after unusual input.
- `screenshots/18.png`: Burp Suite HTTP history.
- `screenshots/22.png`: Additional request and response evidence.

**Security impact:**

SQL injection has not been demonstrated. If a database injection flaw were confirmed, its impact would depend on the vulnerable query and could include unauthorized data access or modification.

**Severity and justification:**

Not assigned. The evidence is insufficient to confirm SQL injection or determine its impact.

**Recommended mitigation:**

- Use parameterized queries and safe query-building mechanisms.
- Validate input on the server without relying on validation as the sole defence.
- Avoid exposing detailed internal exceptions to users.
- Log diagnostic details securely on the server.
- Test relevant database operations using a documented challenge designed for the application's actual backend technology.

**Status:** Not confirmed. An unusual-input response was observed, but SQL injection has not been established.

---

## Finding 5: Sensitive Information Exposure

**Title:** Review of publicly accessible application information

**Category:** Sensitive Information Exposure, Information Disclosure

**Affected feature:** Public About Us and Privacy Policy pages

**Steps performed:**

1. Opened the application's public information pages in Firefox.
2. Inspected the About Us page.
3. Inspected the Privacy Policy page.
4. Observed the information presented through the application's own interface.
5. Reviewed related traffic in Burp Suite HTTP history.

**Expected behaviour:**

Public pages should disclose only information intended for public access. Credentials, authentication tokens, private personal information, and other sensitive internal data should not be exposed through public pages or error responses.

**Actual observed behaviour:**

The screenshots `screenshots/19.png` and `screenshots/21.png` show the About Us and Privacy Policy pages. The visible content includes corporate information text and privacy-related information.

The screenshots do not demonstrate the disclosure of credentials, session tokens, private customer information, or other clearly sensitive data. Publicly accessible policy text is not, by itself, a security vulnerability.

**Evidence filename:**

- `screenshots/19.png`: About Us page.
- `screenshots/21.png`: Privacy Policy page.
- `screenshots/20.png`: Related Burp Suite HTTP history.

**Security impact:**

No sensitive information exposure has been confirmed. Additional evidence would be required to establish that the application exposes information that should be restricted.

**Severity and justification:**

Not assigned. No sensitive disclosure has been demonstrated in the supplied screenshots.

**Recommended mitigation:**

- Minimize information exposed in public responses.
- Never include credentials, API secrets, or session tokens in client-accessible code or public pages.
- Restrict sensitive endpoints using server-side authorization.
- Return generic error messages to users while keeping detailed diagnostics in protected logs.
- Review public responses for unintended data disclosure.

**Status:** Not confirmed. Public information pages were inspected, but no specific sensitive information disclosure was demonstrated.

## Overall Conclusion

The supplied evidence documents a practical security-testing exercise using OWASP Juice Shop and Burp Suite. The search testing produced a browser alert consistent with XSS, which warrants documenting as the primary finding after confirming its execution path.

The basket, authentication, injection, and public-information investigations provide useful testing evidence, but the supplied screenshots do not establish four additional vulnerabilities.&#x20;

## 1. Project Description

The objective of this project was to conduct a practical web application security assessment of OWASP Juice Shop, a n intentionally vulnerable web application designed for security education and authorized penetration-testing pract ice.

The assessment focused on exploring common web application security risks, observing how the application processes  user input, examining authentication behaviour, inspecting HTTP requests and responses, and reviewing the applicati on's publicly accessible information.

The primary investigation areas were:

1. Cross-Site Scripting (XSS)
2. Broken Access Control
3. Authentication Weaknesses
4. Injection and SQL Injection
5. Sensitive Information Exposure

Web application security testing is important because modern applications handle user accounts, personal informatio n, transactions, and other potentially sensitive data. Security weaknesses in input handling, authentication, autho rization, and information disclosure can expose applications to unauthorized access, data manipulation, or other se curity incidents.

Manual testing combined with HTTP traffic inspection helps identify unexpected application behaviour and provides e vidence for evaluating whether a security weakness exists.

The project also provided practical experience with Kali Linux, Firefox, Burp Suite Community Edition, HTTP request  analysis, evidence collection, and technical security reporting.

The assessment was performed as a training exercise against the local Juice Shop instance documented in the supplie d screenshots.

---

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K### 5.1 Finding F-01: Potential Cross-Site Scripting Through Search
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Title:** JavaScript execution associated with crafted search input
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Category:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS)
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Affected feature:** Product search
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Target:** `http://localhost:3000`
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Preconditions:**

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Affected feature:** Login input processing
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Preconditions:**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- The application login form is accessible.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- The tester can submit controlled test input.

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Expected behaviour:**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KInput should be processed safely. Invalid input should not alter the intended database query or authentication logi :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kc, and internal implementation details should not be exposed unnecessarily.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Actual observed behaviour:**

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K### 6.1 Summary of Results

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe assessment documented manual testing of search functionality, login behaviour, basket functionality, public inf :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kormation pages, and HTTP traffic.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe main observations were:
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- A browser alert displaying `xss` was captured during the search-input investigation.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Normal basket functionality was observed.

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Public About Us and Privacy Policy pages were inspected.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Burp Suite screenshots documented selected application traffic.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- The supplied scoreboard screenshot showed four solved challenges at the time of capture.

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe apparent XSS behaviour is the strongest finding in the supplied evidence. It should be retained as a provisiona :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kl finding until its execution path and reproducibility are verified.

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K### 6.3 Limitations

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K3. Broken access control was not demonstrated through an unauthorized resource-access test.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K4. Authentication bypass and ineffective rate limiting were not established.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K5. SQL injection was not confirmed by the single-quote input test.

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K9. The screenshots document the local target. They do not establish that the external demo website was tested.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K10. The assessment should not be considered a comprehensive security certification of the application.

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K## 7. Summary and Recommendations
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K### 7.1 Prioritized Recommendations
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Priority 1: Verify and remediate the apparent XSS behaviour**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe browser alert is the strongest security signal in the supplied evidence.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KRecommended actions:
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Reproduce the original test in the local training application.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Identify the exact input-handling path responsible for script execution.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Determine whether the issue is DOM-based, reflected, or another XSS variant.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Apply context-aware output encoding and safe DOM handling.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Retest the same input and verify that it no longer executes JavaScript.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe provisional severity should be reassessed after confirming exploitability and impact.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Priority 2: Complete authorization testing**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe basket screenshots show ordinary functionality rather than unauthorized access.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KRecommended actions:
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Review server-side ownership checks for protected resources.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Test authorization using separate accounts and controlled test data.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Verify that one account cannot retrieve or modify another account's resources.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Record both the request and the observed authorization result.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Priority 3: Review authentication controls**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe login tests document unsuccessful authentication attempts but do not establish an authentication vulnerability.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KRecommended actions:
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Verify that failed authentication never grants an authenticated session.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Review password verification and session handling.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Test appropriate rate limiting using controlled attempts.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Record any specific weakness only after observing reproducible evidence.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Priority 4: Investigate injection using an appropriate challenge**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe single-quote test did not establish SQL injection.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KRecommended actions:
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Identify the actual backend processing path for the tested input.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Use a documented Juice Shop injection challenge.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Examine the application response and relevant request behaviour.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Avoid classifying generic errors as SQL injection.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Apply parameterized queries and safe input processing where applicable.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Priority 5: Continue reviewing information disclosure**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe public information pages did not demonstrate sensitive data exposure.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KRecommended actions:
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Inspect relevant public responses for unintended sensitive values.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Verify whether any exposed information is genuinely confidential.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Check that secrets are not included in client-accessible resources.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Record the exact exposed data and security impact if a disclosure is found.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K### 7.2 Retesting Strategy
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KAfter remediation, each confirmed issue should be retested using the original reproducible steps.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe retest should document:
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K1. The original test input or request.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K2. The application response before remediation.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K3. The change applied.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K4. The application response after remediation.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K5. Evidence showing whether the issue remains reproducible.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KA finding should be marked as fixed only when the retest demonstrates that the original security failure no longer  :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Koccurs.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K### 7.3 General Security Recommendations
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Adopt secure coding practices for user input and output rendering.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Enforce authorization checks on the server.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Protect authentication and session-management processes.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Use safe database query construction.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Minimize sensitive information in public responses.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Maintain regression tests for identified security issues.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Keep technical evidence and findings traceable to reproducible tests.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K---
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K## 8. Conclusion
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThis project provided practical experience in assessing a deliberately vulnerable web application using Kali Linux, :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K Firefox, and Burp Suite Community Edition.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe assessment covered search functionality, authentication behaviour, basket operations, input handling, public in :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kformation pages, and HTTP request inspection.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe strongest security signal in the supplied evidence was a browser alert displaying `xss` during the search-input :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K investigation. This is consistent with JavaScript execution and warrants further technical verification before fin :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kalizing the finding's subtype and severity.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe other four investigation areas provided useful testing observations, but the supplied screenshots do not establ :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kish additional confirmed vulnerabilities. They therefore remain unconfirmed rather than being reported as successfu :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kl exploits.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe project reinforced several important principles of web application security testing:
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Security claims must be supported by reproducible evidence.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- A test attempt is not equivalent to a confirmed vulnerability.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- HTTP request inspection helps explain application behaviour but does not independently prove exploitability.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Severity should be based on demonstrated impact and realistic exploitability.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Remediation must be verified through retesting.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe assessment demonstrates practical familiarity with manual web application testing, proxy-based HTTP inspection, :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K evidence collection, and structured vulnerability reporting. Further testing and validation would be required to e :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kstablish additional findings and complete a comprehensive security assessment.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K

## 1. Project Description

The objective of this project was to conduct a practical web application security assessment of OWASP Juice Shop, a n intentionally vulnerable web application designed for security education and authorized penetration-testing pract ice.

The assessment focused on exploring common web application security risks, observing how the application processes  user input, examining authentication behaviour, inspecting HTTP requests and responses, and reviewing the applicati on's publicly accessible information.

The primary investigation areas were:

1. Cross-Site Scripting (XSS)
2. Broken Access Control
3. Authentication Weaknesses
4. Injection and SQL Injection
5. Sensitive Information Exposure

Web application security testing is important because modern applications handle user accounts, personal informatio n, transactions, and other potentially sensitive data. Security weaknesses in input handling, authentication, autho rization, and information disclosure can expose applications to unauthorized access, data manipulation, or other se curity incidents.

Manual testing combined with HTTP traffic inspection helps identify unexpected application behaviour and provides e vidence for evaluating whether a security weakness exists.

The project also provided practical experience with Kali Linux, Firefox, Burp Suite Community Edition, HTTP request  analysis, evidence collection, and technical security reporting.

The assessment was performed as a training exercise against the local Juice Shop instance documented in the supplie d screenshots.

---

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K## 3. Implementation Details

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K### 3.1 Environment Setup

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe assessment environment consisted of Kali Linux running in a virtualized environment, with Firefox used to acces :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Ks OWASP Juice Shop and Burp Suite Community Edition used for HTTP inspection.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe application address documented in the evidence was:

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThis address refers to a local application instance. The assessment report therefore distinguishes the local instan :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kce from the separately hosted public demo.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K### 3.2 Burp Suite Configuration
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KBurp Suite Community Edition was opened and configured using its default settings.

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe supplied screenshots document HTTP history and selected requests. This report does not claim that every request :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K was captured or that every HTTPS response was successfully decrypted.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K### 3.3 Cross-Site Scripting Investigation
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe product search feature was examined using ordinary search text and crafted test input.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe supplied screenshot `screenshots/02.png` documents a normal search interaction. `screenshots/03.png` documents  :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kthe crafted search input and resulting search interaction. `screenshots/06.png` shows a browser alert displaying `x :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kss`.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe alert is consistent with JavaScript execution in the tested application. This is the strongest potential vulner :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kability evidence in the supplied material.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe exact XSS subtype and execution path should be verified before final classification. The screenshot does not, b :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Ky itself, demonstrate credential theft, session compromise, or impact on another user.

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K### 5.1 Finding F-01: Potential Cross-Site Scripting Through Search
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Title:** JavaScript execution associated with crafted search input
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Category:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS)
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Affected feature:** Product search
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Target:** `http://localhost:3000`
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Preconditions:**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- The local OWASP Juice Shop application is accessible.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- The tester can interact with the product search field.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Firefox can display the application response.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Steps performed:**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K1. Opened the Juice Shop homepage.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K2. Entered ordinary search text and observed the result.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K3. Entered crafted HTML/JavaScript input into the search field.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K4. Observed the browser's behaviour.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K5. Reviewed the related application traffic and screenshots.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Expected behaviour:**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KUser-supplied search input should be treated as data and rendered safely. It should not be interpreted as executabl :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Ke script.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Actual observed behaviour:**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe screenshot `screenshots/06.png` shows a browser alert displaying `xss` associated with the local Juice Shop app :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Klication. This is consistent with JavaScript execution during the XSS investigation.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe available evidence does not independently establish the precise execution path, whether the issue is DOM-based  :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kor reflected, or whether an attacker could reliably exploit the behaviour against another user's session.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Security impact:**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KIf the behaviour is reproducible and exploitable through an application-accessible input, an attacker may be able t :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Ko execute JavaScript in the application's origin.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KDepending on the affected context and security controls, possible consequences include unauthorized page manipulati :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kon, deceptive interface content, phishing, or access to information available to the injected script.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KNo evidence demonstrates that credentials were stolen, session tokens were compromised, or another user's account w :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kas affected.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Severity and justification:**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Provisional severity: Medium.**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThis provisional rating reflects the apparent script execution and potential consequences of an XSS vulnerability.  :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KA formal severity rating requires confirmation of the execution context, exploitability, affected users, and demons :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Ktrated impact.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Evidence screenshots:**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- `screenshots/02.png`: Normal search interaction.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- `screenshots/03.png`: Crafted search input and search result.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- `screenshots/06.png`: Browser alert displaying `xss`.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- `screenshots/01.png`: Burp Suite HTTP history.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Recommended mitigation:**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K1. Apply context-aware output encoding.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K2. Avoid unsafe DOM APIs that interpret untrusted input as HTML or JavaScript.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K3. Use safe DOM methods such as `textContent` where appropriate.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K4. Sanitize HTML only when HTML rendering is necessary, using a maintained sanitizer.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K5. Implement a restrictive Content Security Policy as defence in depth.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K6. Add regression tests to verify that crafted search input is rendered safely.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Verification and retest result:**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe supplied evidence documents the browser alert but does not contain a complete remediation and retest cycle.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KA retest should repeat the original input after remediation and verify that it is displayed safely without executin :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kg script.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Status:** Provisional. Apparent XSS behaviour documented; exact execution path and reproducibility require verifi :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kcation.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K---
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K### 5.2 Finding F-02: Broken Access Control Investigation
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Title:** Authorization enforcement for basket resources
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Category:** Broken Access Control
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Affected feature:** Shopping basket
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- The Juice Shop application is accessible.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- The tester can access the basket interface.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- A test account and test data are available where required.

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Expected behaviour:**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe server should verify that each user is authorized to view or modify the requested resource. Access to another u :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kser's basket must not be granted solely because a request contains a particular resource identifier.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KNo successful unauthorized access to another user's data, cross-account basket modification, or other authorization :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K bypass is demonstrated.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Security impact:**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KNo security impact from a broken access-control flaw has been established by the supplied evidence.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KIf a resource-level authorization failure were confirmed, possible consequences could include exposure of private b :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kasket information or unauthorized modification of another user's data.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Severity and justification:**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KNot assigned because the required security failure has not been demonstrated.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K

:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K### 6.1 Summary of Results
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe assessment documented manual testing of search functionality, login behaviour, basket functionality, public inf :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kormation pages, and HTTP traffic.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe main observations were:
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- A browser alert displaying `xss` was captured during the search-input investigation.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Normal basket functionality was observed.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Unsuccessful login attempts and application errors were observed.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- A single-quote input test was performed as a preliminary injection investigation.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Public About Us and Privacy Policy pages were inspected.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Burp Suite screenshots documented selected application traffic.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- The supplied scoreboard screenshot showed four solved challenges at the time of capture.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K### 6.2 Investigation Statistics
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K| Metric | Result |
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K|---|---:|
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K| Investigation areas documented | 5 |
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K| Formally verified vulnerabilities | 0 |
[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K| Apparent XSS findings requiring technical verification | 1 |
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K| Other investigation areas not confirmed | 4 |
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K| Confirmed remediation and retest cycles | 0 |
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe distinction between a completed investigation and a confirmed vulnerability is important. A test may be complet :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Ked without demonstrating a security flaw.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe apparent XSS behaviour is the strongest finding in the supplied evidence. It should be retained as a provisiona :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kl finding until its execution path and reproducibility are verified.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K### 6.3 Limitations
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K1. The assessment was limited to the functions and screenshots documented in the supplied evidence.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K2. The precise execution path and subtype of the apparent XSS issue were not fully established.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K3. Broken access control was not demonstrated through an unauthorized resource-access test.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K4. Authentication bypass and ineffective rate limiting were not established.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K5. SQL injection was not confirmed by the single-quote input test.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K6. Sensitive information exposure was not demonstrated through the public information pages.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K7. The scoreboard's solved-challenge count does not independently establish the number of distinct vulnerabilities  :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kconfirmed.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K8. No comprehensive source-code review, automated vulnerability scan, formal CVSS calculation, or remediation retes :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kt is claimed.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K9. The screenshots document the local target. They do not establish that the external demo website was tested.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K10. The assessment should not be considered a comprehensive security certification of the application.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K---
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K## 7. Summary and Recommendations
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K### 7.1 Prioritized Recommendations
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Priority 1: Verify and remediate the apparent XSS behaviour**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe browser alert is the strongest security signal in the supplied evidence.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KRecommended actions:
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Reproduce the original test in the local training application.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Identify the exact input-handling path responsible for script execution.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Determine whether the issue is DOM-based, reflected, or another XSS variant.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Apply context-aware output encoding and safe DOM handling.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Retest the same input and verify that it no longer executes JavaScript.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe provisional severity should be reassessed after confirming exploitability and impact.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Priority 2: Complete authorization testing**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe basket screenshots show ordinary functionality rather than unauthorized access.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KRecommended actions:
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Review server-side ownership checks for protected resources.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Test authorization using separate accounts and controlled test data.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Verify that one account cannot retrieve or modify another account's resources.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Record both the request and the observed authorization result.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Priority 3: Review authentication controls**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe login tests document unsuccessful authentication attempts but do not establish an authentication vulnerability.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KRecommended actions:
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Verify that failed authentication never grants an authenticated session.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Review password verification and session handling.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Test appropriate rate limiting using controlled attempts.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Record any specific weakness only after observing reproducible evidence.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Priority 4: Investigate injection using an appropriate challenge**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe single-quote test did not establish SQL injection.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KRecommended actions:
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Identify the actual backend processing path for the tested input.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Use a documented Juice Shop injection challenge.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Examine the application response and relevant request behaviour.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Avoid classifying generic errors as SQL injection.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Apply parameterized queries and safe input processing where applicable.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K**Priority 5: Continue reviewing information disclosure**
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe public information pages did not demonstrate sensitive data exposure.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KRecommended actions:
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Inspect relevant public responses for unintended sensitive values.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Verify whether any exposed information is genuinely confidential.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Check that secrets are not included in client-accessible resources.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Record the exact exposed data and security impact if a disclosure is found.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K### 7.2 Retesting Strategy
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KAfter remediation, each confirmed issue should be retested using the original reproducible steps.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe retest should document:
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K1. The original test input or request.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K2. The application response before remediation.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K3. The change applied.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K4. The application response after remediation.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K5. Evidence showing whether the issue remains reproducible.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KA finding should be marked as fixed only when the retest demonstrates that the original security failure no longer  :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Koccurs.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K### 7.3 General Security Recommendations
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Adopt secure coding practices for user input and output rendering.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Enforce authorization checks on the server.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Protect authentication and session-management processes.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Use safe database query construction.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Minimize sensitive information in public responses.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Maintain regression tests for identified security issues.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Keep technical evidence and findings traceable to reproducible tests.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K---
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K## 8. Conclusion
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThis project provided practical experience in assessing a deliberately vulnerable web application using Kali Linux, :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K Firefox, and Burp Suite Community Edition.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe assessment covered search functionality, authentication behaviour, basket operations, input handling, public in :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kformation pages, and HTTP request inspection.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe strongest security signal in the supplied evidence was a browser alert displaying `xss` during the search-input :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K investigation. This is consistent with JavaScript execution and warrants further technical verification before fin :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kalizing the finding's subtype and severity.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe other four investigation areas provided useful testing observations, but the supplied screenshots do not establ :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kish additional confirmed vulnerabilities. They therefore remain unconfirmed rather than being reported as successfu :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kl exploits.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe project reinforced several important principles of web application security testing:
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Security claims must be supported by reproducible evidence.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- A test attempt is not equivalent to a confirmed vulnerability.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- HTTP request inspection helps explain application behaviour but does not independently prove exploitability.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Severity should be based on demonstrated impact and realistic exploitability.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K- Remediation must be verified through retesting.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[KThe assessment demonstrates practical familiarity with manual web application testing, proxy-based HTTP inspection, :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K evidence collection, and structured vulnerability reporting. Further testing and validation would be required to e :[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[Kstablish additional findings and complete a comprehensive security assessment.
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K---
:[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K
